*fa-sa-yaʿlamūn* — and the words are v. 3's words, the sūrah's earliest patience-command: "*leave them* — *let them eat, enjoy, and be diverted by hope: soon they will know*" — and the sūrah, which has *midst* its two tellings, placed *exactly* what *that* "soon" *does* (Lot's *muṣbiḥūn*, v. 66; the Ayka's cloud; Thamūd's daybreak, v. 83; and now *the mockers'* Badr, v. 95's *We sufficed you*), returns to the identical clause at its end as a *bookend*: the *soon they will know* of the opening Meccan *nāṣiḥa* has been *proven*, *documented*, *executed* across sixty verses — and v. 96's *fa-sa-yaʿlamūn* is therefore, in the *tafsīr*'s reading, *not a threat but a receipt*: *they will know* the way the towns knew — and the *knowing* will be *the morning's own species*: which is how the sūrah's whole *ʿilm*-theme closes: the *ghulām*'s *ʿalīm* at Abraham's tent (v. 53) — the *ʿilm* of God, *taught*, *gladdening*; "and *We know* that thy breast narrows" (v. 97, next) — the *ʿilm* that *heals*; "*He* is the *knowing* Creator" (v. 86) — the *ʿilm* that *owns* — and *here*: *soon they will know*: the *ʿilm* that *finds*: one root, four directions, and the last is *the warner's*: the God whose *ʿilm* the mockers *portioned off* from His *khalq* will *meet* them as *both*: *khallāqan* and *ʿalīman*: the *making* they imitated and the *knowing* they denied.

**The Present-Tense Polytheists: *yajʿalūna*, and the Verse's Long Reach**

And the verb-form is the verse’s own lesson: the *shirk*-clause is an aorist — *yajʿalūna* — “those who *keep setting up* another god along with God”: not a *made*, once, in the dāhiliyya, but a *making*, daily, continued, the partner re-asserted with every day’s portioning; and beside it the sūra sets v. 91’s perfect — *jaʿalū* — “they *made* the Qur’an into fragments”: *one root of making, two tenses*: the Book chopped *once* — and the rival re-fabricated *every morning*: which is the sūrah’s last warning about *shirk*: it is not an inherited noun but a *renewed verb*, and the *tafsīr*’s moral at v. 96 is therefore *iʿstiqṣḥāb*: the *muʾmin* renews his *lā ilāha illa Llāh* hourly because the *mushrik* renews his partner hourly — and v. 92’s sworn question is sworn, in vv. 91-96, over *both* tenses of *making*: what they *made* of the Book and what they *keep making* beside God. The *sūra*’s whole *faṣl*-theme then closes as it began: the mockers’ *liturgy* — *their* daily *jaʿal*: “God raises by this Book a people and lowers another” — and the *dhikr*’s *sabh*: “he *made* portions; *We* made the seven”: vv. 92-96’s two *kaānu*-liturgies stand side by side for the *mutawassim* of v. 75 to read: the hymn of the *portioners* ends in *fa-sa-yaʿlamūn*; the hymn of the *musabbiḥ* (v. 98) ends in *yaqīn* (v. 99): and the *sūra*’s last word about the *mujrimūn* is the patience-command of v. 96’s opening twin: “*leave them*: … *soon they will know*” (v. 3) — which is how the warner sleeps in Mecca, with *his* ʿibāda, and how the *sūra* sends him out, in its last verses, to *hymn, prostrate, worship — till the certainty*.

---
## Sūrah al-Ḥijr 15:97

> **And certainly We know that thy breast is straitened because of what they say.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"We *Know*" — The Sūrah's Other *la-qad naʿlamu*: the Verse That Arranges History and the Verse That Notices a Heart**

*wa-la-qad naʿlamu annaka qad ḍāqa ṣadru-ka mimma yaqūlūn* — "and *We know*, indeed We know, that thy breast has been straitened by what they say." The reader who has kept the sūrah finds, in this verse's opening, its exact twin from sixty lines above: "*wa-la-qad naʿlamu* the foremost of you and the hindermost We know" (v. 24) — and the two *la-qad naʿlamu*-verses are the sūrah's theodicy in one pair: there, God's knowledge *sorts the umma's ranks across time* — those advanced and those deferred, in the very context of "We give life and death and *We inherit*" (v. 23); here, the same *qad naʿlamu* stoops to *one man's wounded chest in one Meccan afternoon*: the knowledge that arranges all generations *is* the knowledge that counted your griefs. And the tradition sets the verse in the Qur'an's whole consolation-corpus, which the source's note on this verse gathers exactly — "the Prophet is consoled by God for his people's rejection of the message," cross-referenced back to Hūd 11:12c and listed at 5:68; 16:127; 18:6; 26:3; 27:70; 35:8 — whose Meccan law echoes this sūrah's own v. 88 ("grieve not"): the *ḍayq* named, the *ḍayq* known, and the Knower commanding, in the next verse, the cure.

**"Because of What They Say": the Sūrah's Whole Enemy-Action Was Words — and So Is Its Whole Cure**

*mimma yaqūlūn* — and the sūrah's Meccan drama has been, from its first human line, *a war of sayings*: "O you upon whom the Reminder is sent — you are mad!" (v. 6); "if you would bring us the angels" (v. 7); the claim of the veiled hearts — "in our ears is a heaviness, and between us and you a covering" (41:5, the Meccan kin of v. 12's churning doubt); the mob at the door ("did we not forbid you?" v. 70); the *bidʿa*-dividers' label ("fables of the ancients," v. 91's charge); the laughter of v. 95: *the chest was wounded by qawl — and the sūrah, at vv. 98-99, prescribes qawl as the medicine*: "*fa-sabbih* — glorify; *wa-ʿbud* — worship": the *dhikr* answered the *qawāʾil*, sentence for sentence: the *majnūn*-cry (v. 6) met by "We sufficed you" (v. 95), the *covering*-claim (41:5) met by a worship whose organ is the uncovered forehead in *sujūd*. And the handbooks note, at this verse, the *sharḥ al-ṣadr* of the night-journey report — the Prophet's ﷺ chest opened, washed with the water of Zamzam, and *filled with faith and wisdom* (al-Bukhārī, from Anas b. Mālik): the wounded warner is consoled not with a *change of news* but with a *widening of the chest*: the *ḍayq* is met, in this sūrah's economy, by enlargement, not removal — the *ḍābiq* of vv. 49's announcement ("*inform My servants*") is the same *ṣadr* that v. 97's *naʿlamu* has in hand, and the *ghafūr* named there is the *wisdom* poured here.

**"Qad Ḍāqa" — the Present-Perfect: the Grief That Is Happened and Happening, and the *Faʾ* That Follows**

And the tense the verse keeps — *qad ḍāqa* — the perfect with *qad*: *already constricted, and still so*: the grammarians note the courtesy of the form: God does not say *yaḍīqu* (freshly each day, as though the news were new to Him), nor *ḍāqa fa-kashafnāhu* (gone, as though the wound were only memory) — the *state itself* is held, in the sūrah's grammar, inside the *knowing*: it is *real*, and it is *bounded*, both at once. And the *faʾ* of v. 98 (*fa-sabbih*) hangs upon the diagnosis like a hand extended: *I know the chest — therefore, hymn*: the sequence is the sūrah's psychology complete: knowledge precedes prescription, and prescription is not a rebuke of the wound but its *treatment*: the straitened are addressed by a God whose commands *are* the consolation of His prior knowing. With this verse the sūrah's Meccan *ʿilāj* stands finished in four lines — *sufficiency* against the mockers (v. 95), *their* soon-knowing (v. 96), *your* known wound (v. 97), and *your* hymn, prostration, and worship-till-the-end (vv. 98-99) — answering, one by one, the four sorrows of the sent: *ridicule, delay, grief, and exhaustion*: which is the last thing this sūrah tells its preachers: *you will not be told that your people believed; you will be told that your Lord knows* — and the *knowing* is enough, and the *hymn* is next, and the morning (v. 98's hour) is *his*, not the town's.

---
## Sūrah al-Ḥijr 15:98

> **So hymn the praise of thy Lord, and be among those who prostrate.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**The *Fa-sabbih* at the Hour of the *Ṣayḥa*: the Sūrah's Final Counter-Sound**

*fa-sabbih bi-ḥamdi rabbika wa-kun mina's-sājidīn* — "so hymn, with praise of thy Lord, and be among those who bow." The prescription that answers the wounded chest (v. 97) is, in the sūrah's sound-pattern, a *war of two similar sounds*: the towns of this book died to a *ṣayḥa* (vv. 73, 83: *fa-akhāḍhat-humuṣ-ṣayḥatu*) — a *cry* that seized at the *ṣubḥ* — and the warner is sent out, at the sūrah's last hinge, to fill the same mornings with *subḥa*: *tasbīḥ* against *ṣayḥa*, the glorification against the shriek, both in the sūrah's ear at one breath: "We built above you seven strongly-bound... and We guarded them against every rebellious devil; *but whoever snatches a hearing, a piercing flame pursues*" (vv. 16-18), and now the earth's *dhikr* ascends where the *shayāṭīn* were expelled — the sky that *rejects stolen words* is given, at the sūrah's end, its *legitimate* upward speech: *subḥa*: the *dhikr* is the *ḥimā* heaven accepts: and the handbooks at this verse love the pairing of vv. 83 and 98 as the sūrah's final *two mornings*: the *ṣayḥatu muṣbiḥīn* fell on Thamūd at daybreak *once*; the *sabbih* is commanded to the umma at every daybreak *always*: what was a *seizure* for the doomed is an *appointment* for the saved, and the *dhikr* of the dawn-prayer is this sūrah's own *ʿibāda*-clause of vv. 98-99 fulfilled in the *maṣājid* of Islam — recited, in the sūrah's tradition of its *sabʿan min'l-mathānī* (v. 87), *in every unit of every one of those dawns*.

**"With the Praise of Thy Lord": the *Ḥamd* the Sūrah Gave Him at v. 87, Worn as Command at v. 98**

*bi-ḥamdi* — the *bāʾ* of *accompaniment* and *manner* — *hymn, and hymn praising*: and the *ḥamd* is not a new instruction but the *same gift* re-commanded: v. 87 swore "*We have given you the seven of repeated praise (mathānī)*," and the handbooks of the *tafsīr* note that the *seven* are bound up with *al-ḥamdu li-Llāhi rabbi'l-ʿālamīn* (v. 87's source-note transcribes R's line: "God revealed nothing like it in Torah, Gospel, Psalms or Qur'ān — it is the *Mother of the Book* and the seven oft-repeated, *divided between Me and My servant*"): so that v. 98's *fa-sabbih bi-ḥamdi* is the *Fātiḥa's own structure as a life*: *glorify* (the first half — *God praised*), *and praise* (the second — *the servant asking*): the *ḥamd* and the *tasbīḥ* are the *two halves of the sūrah of the Gift*, now *generalized*: "praise Him *and ask*," and the *dhikr* of the whole sūrah — from v. 9's *guarding* to v. 92's *questioning* — is gathered into *the one act of a servant who knows he is heard*: *the wounded chest of v. 97 is told, at v. 98, to do precisely the thing the straitened soul forgets it can do*: *praise while asking*: and the *bāʾ* swears it is *possible*: *praise* is not what the joy *produces* but what the wound *can still perform* — *God is not un-praised by the grieving*: the *bi-ḥamdi* is the sūrah's longest sentence of *permission*.

**"Be Among Those Who Prostrate": the Verse That Re-Answers Iblīs**

*wa-kun mina's-sājidīn* — and this is the moment the whole Ādam-section of the sūrah has been walking toward: at its opening, the angels "*all prostrated together* (*sajada'l-malāʾikatu kulluhum ajmaʿūn*), save Iblīs" (vv. 30-31), and God's question to the refuser rang with the *selfsame phrase*: "*what prevented you from being among those who prostrate* (*allā takūna maʿa's-sājidīn*)" (v. 32) — and now, at the sūrah's end, the command to the warner takes *that* phrase whole: "*be among those who prostrate* (*kun mina's-sājidīn*)": the sūrah's *two* "those-who-prostrate" verses are *its* whole anti-Iblīs *duʿā*: *what the first proud one refused to be, the last prophet is told to be, and to be* ***among*** *them*: the wounded, individual chest (v. 97) is healed by *joining a congregation* (v. 98), as the *āla*-law of vv. 59-60 taught: *salvation in this sūrah is always plural*: and the *kun min* construction — *be of* — is the *tafsīr*'s tenderest verb here: not *make a prostration* but *take your place among the prostrating ones*: the *ṣaff* is the medicine, the *forehead* the receipt: which is why the *sunnah* at this verse added the *mā* of *continuation*: the Prophet's ﷺ own post-prayer *tasbīḥāt* — "*subḥāna Llāh* thirty-three, *al-ḥamdu li-Llāh* thirty-three, *Allāhu akbar* thirty-three at every ṣalāh's closing (Muslim, from ʿAbdullāh b. ʿAmr) — *bi-ḥamdi rabbika* and *mina's-sājidīn*, kept as the umma's *khatma* of every prayer: the sūrah's last command, *made* the *dhikr*'s last custom: *and the sūrah's war on Iblīs ends where his fall began: the back that refused to bend, bowed, and the bowing was counted* ***among***."

---
## Sūrah al-Ḥijr 15:99

> **And worship thy Lord, till certainty comes unto thee.**

**Expanded Commentary**

**"Till the Certainty": the Hour That Comes to *You***

*wa-ʿbud rabbaka ḥattā yaʾtiyaka'l-yaqīn* — "and worship thy Lord *until the certainty comes to you*." The source's note fixes the term (*yaqīn* = death: Q, R, Ṭ, Z — R's reason: "death's inevitable nature," Ṭ's: "with death one attains certainty of all that is promised in the Hereafter"), and the sūrah's own *mīzān* of the last two verses shows why the word had to be *al-yaqīn* and not *al-mawt*: this is the sūrah of the *mawāʿīd* — "their tryst is the morning," *wa-mawʿiduhumu'ṣ-ṣubḥ* (v. 66's law, 11:81) — and every town in it perished at a *time that came to it*, not a time it chose: Lot's morning *fell*; Thamūd's Cry *seized them at daybreak*; and now the Prophet's own *morning* is named the same way: *ḥattā yaʾtiyaka*: *it comes to you*: the private *ṣayḥa* of the individual death enters the sūrah's last clause exactly like the public ones entered its stories — *as a visitor with a known master*: the *dhikr*'s God who *sent* the mornings sends the *yaqīn*, and the verb's direction ("comes to you," not "you reach") is the verse's whole *adab* of death: *it is not an achievement of the dying but an appointment of the Living*: "what the towns *used to earn availed them not*" (v. 84) — *only what they were told to do, and did, till it came*.

**"Worship": the Sūrah's Last *ʿAbd*, Answering Its Last Announcement**

*wa-ʿbud rabbaka* — and the sūrah's final command is the echo of its final *announcement*: at vv. 49-50 the Lord dictated, in the first person of His *names*: "*inform My servants that I — I am* the All-Forgiving, the All-Merciful, *and that My punishment — it is* the abiding scourge": and v. 99 now puts in the *servant's* mouth the *ʿibāda* that lives between those two announcements — *worship*, in the time the *ghafūr* and the *ʿadhāb* are both true: the *ʿibād* (v. 49) and the *ʿabd* (v. 99) are the sūrah's bracket of mercy: *God's news about Himself is answered by man's act about his Lord*, and the act is *given no deadline but one*: which is the sūrah's last *tawḥīd*-stroke in its grammar of address: *rabbaka*, *thy Lord*, for the fourth time running in its closing verses (vv. 86, 92, 98, 99): *thy* — the sūrah that ended its town-catalogue with *intiqām* and its Meccan quarrel with *kifāya* leaves its Prophet, and every reader, in the *rabb* relation alone: *no more names needed for the worship*: the *dhikr* of v. 9, *guarded by God*, has *guarded down*, verse by verse, to the *dhikr* of v. 99, *kept by the servant*: the two guardianships meet in one command: *keep worshipping, and I will keep the rest*.

**The Certainty of the Station: the Second Reading, the *Voluntary Death*, and the Sūrah's Send-Off**

Beside the *yaqīn*-as-death stands, in the source's note, the *yaqīn* of the *ʿārifūn*: "the *station where the worshipper is with the Worshipped and knows God with certainty* (Bq, K), so that *nothing distracts from the fullness of worship*" — the Qur'ān's own phrase for which, "the *truth of certainty*" (*ḥaqq al-yaqīn*, 56:95; 69:51; 102:5's *ʿilm* of it), the note cites — and the Sufi reading the source transcribes from al-Sulamī binds the two: "*to be certain of the truth is to die to falsehood, since nothing of one's being can dwell any longer in falsehood*; in this state one *sees nothing but God and converses with none but Him*: this kind of death, characterized by the station of certainty (*maqām al-yaqīn*), is what is known in Islamic thought as *the voluntary death*" — *mūtū qabla an tamūtū*, the gnostics' famous motto — and the sūrah *permits* this reading by the very *word* it chose: *al-yaqīn*, the *nomen* of *ʿayn*: certainty that *sees* (Ṭ's reason above: *with death one attains certainty of what is promised*): so that vv. 98-99's *ʿibāda* "until the certainty" is, in the two readings at once, a *single sentence with two horizons*: *worship on, till the Hour that is yours arrives — or till, before it arrives, you arrive at what it shows*: and the *dhikr* of the *voluntary death* — dying to the *kasb* that avails not (v. 84), to the *āminīn* hewn from rock (v. 82), to the *ḍayq* of the counted griefs (v. 97) — is the sūrah's whole prior vocabulary inverted: *everything it destroyed in the towns, it commands dying-from in the servant, before the dying-to*: "so be among those who prostrate" was the posture, "and worship *till the certainty*" is the *career*: and with this last clause the sūrah of the *Ḥijr* — whose *rocks* the condemned hewed and whose *Morning* the saved outrode — hands the reader his one *non-hewn* house: *the ʿibāda that will be found standing when everything carved is fallen*: the *dhikr* was guarded (v. 9); the warner was sufficed (v. 95); the chest was known (v. 97); and *the worshipper*: *worship till it comes*, and *it will come*: *and this is the sūrah's final word about tomorrow to every man on the road*: **the certainty has an Owner, and He is the Khalq and ʿIlm of v. 86, the Ghafūr and Raḥīm of v. 49: worship Him, and leave the coming to the Coming.**

---
**[End of the commentary on Sūrah al-Ḥijr]**
